  - **It says where it is as it goes,** on the host's progress lines, as `load` does: loading, meshing and checking the bodies, ruling pairs out by their bounds, and "N pairs: ..., E to read exactly; reading the E exactly: K read", said again every 5 s. A command stopped at its limit now says the last of these after "it ran past the 600 s limit and was stopped. Where it was: ...", from the harness's box and from `scripts/bench.py` both.
  - **A whole assembly still ran past 600 s** with the bounds: once they had ruled out nearly all its pairs, the few hundred left took several seconds each to read exactly. The plan's estimate timed the first 3 of those, which were cheaper than the rest. So:
  - **Each exact read says where its time goes,** step by step, in its `seconds`: the mesh's overlap (Manifold, fine and coarse), OCCT's distance, its common and cut, the mesh's gap, and on an STL the sections and the sampled points, with places. `numbers.exact_s` sums them over the pairs read exactly, and `--plan` over the ones it times, which are spread through the pairs read exactly, not the first.
  - **The mesh's gap is searched only as far as agreeing with OCCT's asks:** OCCT's distance, plus the chords on both and a micron. It was searched to OCCT's distance plus 1 mm, and the search costs as the triangles within it. The figure is the same wherever the two agree. Where the mesh reads more than that, it's `DISAGREE` as before, said as "the mesh's over ...". On an STL it's searched as far as the sampled points' gap, which is never less than the mesh's: the same figure, sooner.
  - **OCCT's common and cut aren't run on a pair two other methods read apart:** OCCT's exact distance, past the chords on both, and the mesh's gap, past them too, with no overlap by the mesh, fine or coarse. OCCT's distance reads 0 for one solid wholly inside another (its inner solution), so it reads whether any material is shared, not only where the surfaces are. Each boolean then reads 0, as for a pair whose boxes are apart, and the pair says so in `occt_booleans` and in its second check. A pair that meets, or is near enough for the chords to matter, is read by all three, as before: near-touching faces are where OCCT's booleans go wrong, and where the three are needed.
  - **OCCT's distance runs on the container's CPUs, and for its minimum alone,** on OCCT's thread pool sized to the container's quota (`cpu.max`, 2): OCCT counts the machine's, 8 in a 2-CPU container, so it ran 8 threads on 2 and skewed the timings the limit is taken from. It's about half the time on curved faces, and bit for bit the same figure and the same nearest points, run after run, as one thread reading both extrema. On four NURBS pairs, touching, 0.21 mm apart, 17.7 mm apart and against a cap meeting 123 times, 20 runs each, on 8 threads and again on 2, were identical to the bit and to the one-thread read, 0.12 to 0.17 s against 0.26 to 0.32.
  - **OCCT's volumes are integrated adaptively** (`VOLUME_PRECISION`, a relative 1e-9), for `common` and for both volumes `cut` subtracts. A whole assembly read `DISAGREE` where a flat washer sat on a printed flange: OCCT's `cut` read its overlap under nothing, past the ±0.001 mm³ band. OCCT's default volume is a fixed-order integration, off by about 1e-5 of the whole on B-spline faces, as a CAD export's are, so `cut`, the part's volume less the part with the washer cut away, read a touching contact as a negative overlap. Made up, a washer 3.5 mm out and 0.5 mm thick seated round a hole in a plate turned 37.3° about z, touching: on planar faces `cut` reads 0; on the same plate as NURBS, −0.41 mm³ for a 40 mm plate, −0.89 for 120 mm and −1.61 for 240 mm. Integrated to 1e-9 it reads 0.000000 on all of them, in a few milliseconds a body. The band is unchanged: a real overlap reads as `common` does, half of a 1 mm cube 0.498877 mm³ and a sliver 0.05 mm deep 0.108605 mm³ by both. `tests/test_clearance_far.py` reads the 120 mm plate's washer `TOUCHING`, its cut 0, and a 1 mm cube half in it `INTERFERE`, 0.5 mm³ by both.
  - **A reading that will run long takes up a longer limit, and states it** (`bench/pace.py`). The host offers `clearance` the hour, as it offers `load` two passes. `clearance` reads its exact pairs in an order spread through them, about 0.618 of the way on each time, and after each one projects its whole time from those read so far. Where that passes its limit, less 20 s to write a reply, it takes up the offer with `load`'s line and states its own limit: 1.5 times the projection, never under the host's and never over the hour. It raises the stated limit as its pairs come slower, up to the hour. A reading that fits keeps 600 s. The reply's `limit:` line and `numbers.limit` say what was taken and why. Whatever limit it's under, it stops itself 20 s before it (a tenth of it, if that's less), `NO ANSWER`, saying how many of its exact pairs it read and how long each took, so a reply is written rather than the command killed; a reading with no pair left to read is never stopped. Its clock is the process's age by the kernel (`/proc/self/stat`), as the host's limit runs from the container's start and the reading's own modules take seconds to import. `--plan` says the limit it would be under, and times its 3 reads under the same limit, stopping where it would run past it, saying so.
  - *Made up (`tests/test_clearance_far.py`):* a tube 4 mm round swept along a spline and a copy beside it, about 1 mm apart: the mesh bound reads the fine mesh, each body padded at least twice what its triangulation says it's off, where the coarse one it first read says 0.0496 mm against a pad of 0.05; judged 0.005 mm past their exact gap they're read exactly, `TOO CLOSE`, and judged 0.005 mm short, `CLEAR`, never by a bound past the gap; and OCCT's pool runs as many threads as the container's CPUs.
  - *Made up (`tests/test_clearance_far.py`):* the 24-slice ring told its limit is 1 s, and offered the hour: it takes it up after its first exact pair, states its limit, the host waits for it, and it reads `TOUCHING`, as it does under 600 s. Told 1 s with nothing offered, it stops itself after its first exact pair, `NO ANSWER`, saying so. The rod through the ring, read exactly, has its common and cut not run, and the buried pin has them run, and they find its 1 mm³.
  - *Made up (`tests/test_clearance_far.py`):* a ring 3 to 6 mm round a rod of 1.5 mm, 1.5 mm of play, a 1 mm pin wholly inside a block, and a plate far off, 10 pairs. The rod's box and hull lie through the ring's, so the meshes rule the pair out, at their 0.5 mm search, where `--exact` reads 1.5 mm; the buried pin's surface is 4.5 mm from the block's, and it's read exactly, `INTERFERE`, 1 mm³, as `--exact` reads it. `--plan` gives the same split as the reading.
  - *Made up (`tests/test_clearance_far.py`):* a 15° slice of a flat ring 20 to 40 mm out, 5 mm thick, with a 3 mm hole through its middle, placed 24 times round the ring, 276 pairs. The 24 neighbours are read exactly, `TOUCHING`, and the 252 others by their hulls, the least bound gap 5.121 mm where the exact least is 5.2208; the verdict and every exactly read pair's figures are `--exact`'s own, and each bound gap is no more than its pair's exact gap. On the Mac the pairs took 0.80 s, against 3.84 s with `--exact`. With `--min-gap 6` the 24 pairs 5.22 mm apart are read exactly, `TOO CLOSE` as with `--exact`; a `--box` round one seam is read exactly on the one pair it catches; and `--max-gap` reads every pair exactly.

*Made up, in the first image and the second (`tests/test_placed.py`):* a plate 60 × 60 × 4 mm with a 40 mm pocket from z 1 to 3 under a 36 mm window each way, and its pane, 0.1 mm of play all round; and a given block, `references/leg.step`, x 30 to 50, y −10 to 10, z 60 to 140. Built flat, the plate passes the harness's `light`, `capture` and `pause` at `done`. Placed by `[["x", 90]]` and `[0, 0, 100]` it stands from y −4 to 0 and z 70 to 130, and `clearance` reads it `TOUCHING` the block at x 30; unplaced it's `CLEAR`, 56 mm below; placed at `[10, 0, 100]` it's `INTERFERE`, 2400 mm³, the box x 30 to 40, y −4 to 0, z 70 to 130. `fit-on` along −x reads that one `BLOCKED` and the unplaced one `CLEAR`.

*Made up (`tests/test_placed_many.py`):* a 30° slice of a flat ring 20 to 40 mm out and 5 mm thick, built centred, and a 4 mm pane on it. Three parts turn it about z to 0°–30°, 30°–60° and 90°–120°, its `at` to six decimals, so the faces two neighbours meet by are off the axes and a hair apart (mqucifer/print-shop#334). `clearance slice.step` reads it three times, three pairs: the neighbours `TOUCHING`, and the others `CLEAR`, 10.353 mm and 20.000 mm at their inner corners; `--part s1 s3` reads the one pair. Two neighbours each with the pane in its `with` read six pairs, each slice touching its neighbour and its own pane. `render` draws the three in three colours, each seen. `fit-on` moving the third `--as s3` in along −x reads `BLOCKED` on the other two, never on itself, and without `--as` is `NO ANSWER`; so are `clearance --slide` and `--lift` of the ring, which with `--as` move that part alone. A `done` on two given files each in three parts is met, its need and the harness's placed reading each reading the six parts; and a held given strip named in two parts has its way on read once for each, `--as` each.

## Limits

- **Docker's disk is shared,** with LiteLLM's database, crew's workspaces and any running tick, and it filled on 2026-11-01. There are two images. The second, `print-shop/cae:0.2` (mqucifer/print-shop#142), which runs `load` too with nothing added to it (mqucifer/print-shop#156), and `optimise` with BESO's five Python files and its licence added in a layer of their own, 475 kB (mqucifer/print-shop#286), is 3.20 GB on the Mac, of at most 4 GB: micromamba 2.0.7 by its digest (not 2.0.5, whose libcurl could abort an install on a descriptor it closed twice, mqucifer/print-shop#307), and conda-forge's FreeCAD 1.1.3, CalculiX 2.23, gmsh 4.15.2 and what they need, 296 packages on arm64 and 306 on amd64, each by its version, build and MD5 (`bench/cae-image/lock-*.txt`), so a build solves nothing. `build cae` refuses if it, and the first image where that isn't built yet, could leave under 5 GB free, and `build` counts the second the same way; `check` holds each to its limit and names any other tag of the second left on the machine. The first, `print-shop/bench:0.15`, is 2.13 GB, of at most 4 GB. It holds what 0.2 did, with the bench's version as a label: the bench code is mounted, so 0.3 marks the bench that reads islands and holes (mqucifer/print-shop#27), 0.4 the one with `light` and `thickness` (mqucifer/print-shop#37), 0.5 the `capture` that says what the frame lacks over or under a pane (mqucifer/print-shop#46), 0.6 the `info` that reads small flat faces, the `capture` that names other plays and the sheet that marks files by name (mqucifer/print-shop#54), 0.7 the enclosure's `edges` and `clearance --max-gap` (mqucifer/print-shop#60), the needs that follow the part's files and the check for curves stored as strips (mqucifer/print-shop#59), and `layout` and `capture`'s rim figures (mqucifer/print-shop#61), 0.8 `clearance --lift` and its verdict on OCCT's exact gap, `edges` by group and by place, and `capture`'s and `rays`' clearer replies (mqucifer/print-shop#65), and the refinement pass's turn onto the side the run names and its reading of gap fill inside a wall (mqucifer/print-shop#66), 0.9 `clearance`'s gaps by place and one rule for a figure at its limit (mqucifer/print-shop#72), 0.10 `clearance --slide` and `HELD` (mqucifer/print-shop#76), 0.11 the replies that say where, `clearance`'s bodies and overlaps, `light`'s, `thin`'s and `capture`'s, and `faces` and `slice` (mqucifer/print-shop#77), 0.12 the slide that reads a contact where the parts already touch and keeps a verdict both methods agree on (mqucifer/print-shop#84), and the `info` that counts runs of strips, not narrow flat faces (mqucifer/print-shop#85), 0.13 networkx, which trimesh needs to split a mesh whose body has an open seam, with the slide's `HELD BY A SEPARATE BODY` (mqucifer/print-shop#122), 0.14 the slide's tie round the part (mqucifer/print-shop#135) and `wall --z` (mqucifer/print-shop#136), and 0.15 the slide that follows a widening contact back by its curve (mqucifer/print-shop#143) and the `info` that stacks an STL's sections between its level faces (mqucifer/print-shop#144). Each up to 0.12 shares all of 0.2's layers; 0.13 shares them up to the bench's own install, whose new pin builds that layer and the ones after it again, 2.13 GB in all, and 0.14 and 0.15 share all of 0.13's (0.1 had no `lookup` or `docs` indexes; any of them may still be on a machine). `build` refuses if that could leave under 5 GB free, and `check` says if the image or the disk is over. Its first layers are mqucifer/model-performance's CadQuery image, so where that's present it costs tens of MB.
- **A container**, of either image, has no network, 2 CPUs, 3 GiB with no swap, a read-only image, 1 GB of `/tmp`, the invoking user, no capabilities, no log on Docker's disk, a 2 GiB limit on any file, and 600 s, an hour for `optimise`, and an hour for `load` where it reads in two passes (above). `/work` is the only thing it writes, and the bench's code is mounted read-only, so changing the code never costs another image. Every package is pinned.
- **It moves to another machine** by clone and `build`: CI builds it for amd64 and runs the planted cases there, and the Mac builds it for arm64.
- **Accuracy:** the mesh is at 0.002 mm, and a figure is good to the band it prints. Nothing here has been compared with a print. A pause layer assumes a layer takes its outline from its mid-height. Orca has since agreed with it on our fixtures and on three approved runs (the refinement pass, mqucifer/print-shop#51), except at a tie: a ceiling on a layer's mid-height, where `pause` closes the pocket a layer early and Orca leaves it open. The Centauri's mid-print pause hasn't been run.
- **`bench/refine.py`** isn't a command: it's the refinement pass's geometry, started by the harness. It meshes STEP for Orca at Orca's own GUI defaults and holds the mesh to OCCT, and reads Orca's lines against the part.

## Tests

Planted fixtures are made from scratch by `tests/bench_cases/make_fixtures.py`, with right answers from arithmetic: `uv run pytest -q -m bench`. `tests/test_pieces.py` draws its own pictures and maps (`bench_cases/planted_pieces.py`): a clean panel, one with one of each rule break, a curved scene and two maps as rules would write them. `tests/test_pieces_local.py` uses three more of them, a panel with a dark patch, one with half a line faint, and a band of lead between three pieces, for the fixes that work on one place. `tests/test_pieces_dome.py` writes its own bands files for a dome of the planned size, and bends each slice back by its file's angles in plain Python, against the ellipse. `tests/test_pieces_seams.py` builds two neighbours with seams, their came strips and rings, and places them, with each angle worked out from the rows' corners by cross products; its controls are two slices built square-edged, a neighbour built for another gap, a lip that leaves a groove too near an opening, today's H strip over the outer face, a bar over an opening, a strip made for another gap, and rings made for a thinner frame. The second image's are made in FreeCAD by `tests/cae_cases/make_fixtures.py`: `uv run pytest -q -m cae`, which needs both images, as one test follows the slide's reply into `fit-on` and `bend`. `load`'s cases are there too, and `fit-bed`'s in the first image's. The cases of a whole furniture assembly (mqucifer/print-shop#295) are marked `assembly` as well, and CI runs them in a job of their own, `cae-assembly`, as they take about 23 minutes of solves, on the same image as `cae`, which `cae-image` builds once per run (mqucifer/print-shop#307): `-m "cae and assembly"` runs them alone, and `-m cae` still runs every one. Those of a glass frame's band under `--parts` and a lift (mqucifer/print-shop#375) are marked `bands` too, and run in `cae-bands`, beside it, as `cae-assembly` is near its 30 minutes. `tests/test_load.py` needs no image: the assembly file, the bought parts' tables, a through-bolt's hole and its nut's pocket read from plain rows of faces, a dovetail's joint in the file and its faces' pushes against a wedge worked by hand (mqucifer/print-shop#170), the arithmetic that shares a joint's force between its bolts, statics across a joint, an element's forces, and the furniture toolset. No Sponsor's design, or anything derived from one, is in this repository, and `scripts/bench_local_run.py` refuses to write inside it. `tests/test_bench_local.py` (`-m bench_local`) runs on the Sponsor's exported frame where it's present, and skips elsewhere. The frame's heights and play are in `~/print-shop/bench-local.json` on the Mac, off this repository. `scripts/bench_local_run.py triforce --out DIR` saves that run's figures to a folder off the repository, and `reference --out DIR` runs the commands over our reference parts in mqucifer/model-performance.
